      "text": "Article (51)\nEnd-of-Service Gratuity for Full-Time Workers\n1. The national Worker is entitled to an end-of-service gratuity upon the end of his service,\nin accordance with the legislation regulating pensions and social security in force in the\nState.\n2. The foreign full-time Worker, who has completed one or more years of continuous service,\nis entitled to an end-of-service gratuity upon the end of his service, calculated according\nto the basic wage, as follows:\na. The wage of twenty-one (21) days for each year of the first five years of service.\nb. The wage of thirty (30) days for each year in excess of that.\n3. The foreign Worker is entitled to a bonus for parts of the year in proportion to the amount\nspent in work, provided that he has completed one year of continuous service.\n4. The days of absence from work without pay shall not be included in the calculation of the\nduration of service.\n5. Without prejudice to what is decided by legislation regarding the granting of pensions or\nretirement benefits to Workers in some Establishments, the end-of-service gratuity shall\nbe calculated according to the last basic wage received by the foreign Worker, for those\nwho receive their wages by month, week, or day, and according to the average daily wage\nstipulated in the provisions of this Decree by law for those who receive their wages by\npiece-meal.\n6. The foregoing is provided that the total end-of-service gratuity for the foreign Worker does\nnot exceed two years' wage."

      "text": "Article (51)\nEnd-of-Service Gratuity for Full-Time Workers\n1. The national Worker is entitled to an end-of-service gratuity upon the end of his service,\nin accordance with the legislation regulating pensions and social security in force in the\nState.\n2. The foreign full-time Worker, who has completed one or more years of continuous service,\nis entitled to an end-of-service gratuity upon the end of his service, calculated according\nto the basic wage, as follows:\na. The wage of twenty-one (21) days for each year of the first five years of service.\nb. The wage of thirty (30) days for each year in excess of that.\n3. The foreign Worker is entitled to a bonus for parts of the year in proportion to the amount\nspent in work, provided that he has completed one year of continuous service.\n4. The days of absence from work without pay shall not be included in the calculation of the\nduration of service.\n5. Without prejudice to what is decided by legislation regarding the granting of pensions or\nretirement benefits to Workers in some Establishments, the end-of-service gratuity shall\nbe calculated according to the last basic wage received by the foreign Worker, for those\nwho receive their wages by month, week, or day, and according to the average daily wage\nstipulated in the provisions of this Decree by law for those who receive their wages by\npiece-meal.\n6. The foregoing is provided that the total end-of-service gratuity for the foreign Worker does\nnot exceed two years' wage."
    },

The annual number of working hours stipulated in the employment contract divided by the\nannual number of working hours under a full-time contract, multiplied by 100 equals the\npercentage multiplied by the value of the end of service pay under a full-time employment\ncontract.\n2. The end of service pay shall not be applicable in the event of operating under a temporary\nemployment model or if its duration is less than a year."

      "text": "Article (47)\nUnlawful Termination of Worker's Service\n1. The termination of the Worker's service by the Employer shall be unlawful if the\ntermination of the Worker's service is due to his filing a serious complaint to the Ministry\nor filing a case against the Employer that has been proven to be true.\n2. The Employer is obligated to pay a fair compensation to the Worker estimated by the\ncompetent court, if it is proven that the dismissal is unlawful in accordance with Clause\n(1) of this Article. The amount of compensation shall be determined taking into account\nthe kind of work, the amount of damage incurred by the Worker and the duration of his\nservice. In all cases, the amount of compensation must not exceed the wage of the Worker\nfor a period of three (3) months, calculated according to the last wage received thereby.\n3. The provisions of Clause (2) of this Article shall not prejudice the right of the Worker to\nthe warning allowance and the end-of-service gratuity entitled thereto in accordance with\nthe provisions of this Decree by law."

And in\nthe event that he wishes to return to the State and obtain a new work permit within three\n(3) months as of the date of leaving, the new Employer shall pay the compensation\nstipulated in Clause (3) of this Article, unless agreed otherwise between the Worker and\nthe original Employer.\n5. If either party terminates the employment contract without observing the provisions of\nthis Article, it shall pay the second party a compensation equal to the Worker's wage for\nthe notice period or the remaining period of the notice period.\n6. If the foreign worker leaves the State without being bound by the provisions of this article,\nhe will not be granted a work permit to work in the State for a period of one year as of the\ndate of leaving the State.\n7. If the foreign Worker is absent from work, for no legitimate reason, before the end of the\ncontract period, no other work permit shall be granted thereto to join other work in the\nState in accordance with the provisions of this Decree by law, for a period of one year as\nof the date of the absence from work. Employers must rectify their situation and convert indefinite-term employment contracts\ninto fixed-term employment contracts, in accordance with the conditions, controls and\nprocedures stipulated in this Decree by law within one year as of the date of its entry into\nforce; and the Minister may extend this period for other periods as required by the public\ninterest.\n3.     {
      "id": "executive-regulations:en:30:1",
      "instrument": "executive-regulations",
      "article": "30",
      "text": "Article (30)\nEnd of Service Pay for Workers Subject to Other Work Models\nSubject to the provisions of Article (52) of the Decree-Law, the end of service pay for workers\noperating under a part-time or employment sharing model, and not under a full-time model, shall\nbe calculated as follows:\n1. The annual number of working hours stipulated in the employment contract divided by the\nannual number of working hours under a full-time contract, multiplied by 100 equals the\npercentage multiplied by the value of the end of service pay under a full-time employment\ncontract.\n2. The end of service pay shall not be applicable in the event of operating under a temporary\nemployment model or if its duration is less than a year."

Subject to the provisions of Article (7) of the Decree-Law, a worker may be employed under\nany of the work models stipulated in the mentioned article, with the following additional\nmodels:\na. Remote work: the work is performed wholly or partially outside the workplace and the\nconnection between the worker and the employer shall be electronic instead of the\nphysical presence – whether the work was parttime or fulltime.\nb. Subject to the provisions stipulated in the Decree-Law, the worker and employer shall abide\nby the provisions stipulated in the employment contract in accordance with every model of\nthose mentioned in clause (1) of this article."

Full-time, which is working for one employer for the full daily working hours\nthroughout business days.\nb. Part-time, which is working for one or multiple employers for a specified number of\nworking hours or days.\nc. Temporary work, which is work of which nature requires a specific period of time, or\nwhich focuses on a specific job and ends by the completion thereof.\nd. Flexible work, which is work of which hours or working days change according to the\nvolume of work and the economic and operational variables of the Employer. Work Permit (Recruitment of workers from outside the State): This type of work\npermits enables establishments registered with the Ministry to recruit a worker from\noutside the State.\nb. Work Permit for Transfer: The permit under which a non-national worker can transfer\nfrom and to an establishment registered with the Ministry.\nc. Permit for those who are sponsored by their parents: The permit under which those\nwho are sponsored by their parents may be employed to work at an establishment\nregistered with the Ministry.\nd. Temporary Work Permit: The permit under which a worker is employed for a job, whose\nnature requires a limited period for its execution or completion at one of the\nestablishments registered with the Ministry.\ne. Work Permit for a Mission: A permit that enables one of the establishments registered\nwith the Ministry, willing to recruit a worker from abroad to complete a temporary work\nor a fixed project with a determined period.\nf. Part-time, which is working for one or multiple employers for a specified number of\nworking hours or days.\nc.       "text": "Article (51)\nEnd-of-Service Gratuity for Full-Time Workers\n1. The national Worker is entitled to an end-of-service gratuity upon the end of his service,\nin accordance with the legislation regulating pensions and social security in force in the\nState.\n2. The foreign full-time Worker, who has completed one or more years of continuous service,\nis entitled to an end-of-service gratuity upon the end of his service, calculated according\nto the basic wage, as follows:\na. The wage of twenty-one (21) days for each year of the first five years of service.\nb. The wage of thirty (30) days for each year in excess of that.\n3. The foreign Worker is entitled to a bonus for parts of the year in proportion to the amount\nspent in work, provided that he has completed one year of continuous service.\n4. The days of absence from work without pay shall not be included in the calculation of the\nduration of service.\n5. Without prejudice to what is decided by legislation regarding the granting of pensions or\nretirement benefits to Workers in some Establishments, the end-of-service gratuity shall\nbe calculated according to the last basic wage received by the foreign Worker, for those\nwho receive their wages by month, week, or day, and according to the average daily wage\nstipulated in the provisions of this Decree by law for those who receive their wages by\npiece-meal.\n6. The foregoing is provided that the total end-of-service gratuity for the foreign Worker does\nnot exceed two years' wage."
    },
    {
      "id": "labour-law:en:51:2",
      "instrument": "labour-law",
      "article": "51",
      "text": "7. The Cabinet may, upon the proposal of the Minister and after coordination with the\nConcerned Authorities, approve other alternative systems for the end-of-service gratuity\nsystem, and the decision issued thereby shall specify the conditions, controls and\nmechanism of contribution to these systems."

      "text": "Article )7(\nTypes of Work\n1. The work types to be contracted shall be according to the following:\na. Full-time, which is working for one employer for the full daily working hours\nthroughout business days.\nb. Part-time, which is working for one or multiple employers for a specified number of\nworking hours or days.\nc. Temporary work, which is work of which nature requires a specific period of time, or\nwhich focuses on a specific job and ends by the completion thereof.\nd. Flexible work, which is work of which hours or working days change according to the\nvolume of work and the economic and operational variables of the Employer. The\nWorker may work for the Employer for times that change according to work\nconditions and requirements.\ne. Any other types specified by the Executive Regulation of this Decree by law.\n2. The Executive Regulation shall determine the conditions and controls for work types, and\nthe obligations of both the worker and the employer, according to each type."

30. final_answer

{
  "type": "final_answer",
  "turn": 6,
  "answer": "Under the Labour Law and its Executive Regulations, no end-of-service gratuity is due on the facts you gave.\n\nWhy: your project-based engagement is a \"temporary work\" model. Article (7)(1)(c) of the Labour Law defines temporary work as work whose nature requires a specific period, or which focuses on a specific job and ends on its completion. The gratuity for work types other than full-time is left by Article (52) of the Labour Law to the Executive Regulations, and Article (30)(2) of the Executive Regulations states that end-of-service pay \"shall not be applicable in the event of operating under a temporary employment model or if its duration is less than a year.\" So the temporary model is excluded from end-of-service pay.\n\nFor completeness: the gratuity formula in Article (51) of the Labour Law (21 days' basic wage per year for the first five years, 30 days per year thereafter, capped at two years' wage) applies to the foreign full-time worker. If your engagement were in fact a full-time model rather than a temporary one, the calculator would produce AED 9,312.04 for 1 Jan 2023 – 30 Jun 2024 on a basic wage of AED 9,000. But because you describe a temporary contract, Article (30)(2) of the Executive Regulations means that figure does not apply to you.\n\nThis is not legal advice. If you are unsure which work model your contract and permit actually record, the Ministry of Human Resources and Emiratisation or a lawyer can confirm it from those documents.",
